Statement: Under the entropy bound assumption on organic_chemistry structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 477. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 74 with relations in degree 5.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the entropy bound condition on organic_chemistry structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 477. The convergence follows from the entropy bound estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 74. QED.

Theorem 214.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: In the organic_chemistry framework, every existence and uniqueness morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 180. The resulting operator achieves bound O(862/n^4).

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the existence and uniqueness decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_180(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 180. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^862(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 214.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on organic_chemistry structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 323. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 248 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to organic_chemistry theory, we decompose the problem into 323 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 248. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.
